# M0 device and human-review runbook

For the product owner, to run on your own devices. It gathers every physical-device and human step that Stage 0 (M0) still needs from the eight device checklists in [`device-checklists/`](device-checklists/) and from every pending-device and pending-human row of the eight gate reports, and puts them into as few sittings as possible, in the order that unblocks the M0 gate first (plan Sections 21.1, 21.2 and 22.4).

- **Where a step here and its checklist step differ, the checklist step wins.** If you find a difference, follow the checklist and note it in the session log. Two checks keep the two in line, and neither covers every expected result (Section 9's last row describes what each compares and what it does not): `runbook-coverage.py` checks step numbers only (every checklist step classified in Section 7, every blocking one in a session), and `runbook-content-check.py` compares markers in both directions: texts in double quotes, texts in backticks that hold a log field or a count, and three kinds of count written in prose (tests run, tests executed, files read back). Each checklist step's markers must appear somewhere in the runbook's header or Sections 1-7, not necessarily in the matching step, unless they belong to a checklist step the runbook leaves to its checklist (the whole of G0.4 step 1 and of G0.5 AI step 9: 31 markers at this commit) or are listed in the check as not carried; and each of the runbook's in the checklist of the gate its step names, or be listed in the check as the runbook's own, most of them with a source the check reads. It does not compare a status or message written in backticks without a field or a count (such as 4.4's `pairing failed: pinMismatch`), other numbers written in prose, or meaning: for those, the checklist is the reference. How the runbook came to differ from the checklists, and what was carried in, is at the start of Section 8.
- **Nothing in this runbook has been run on a device.** Every step stays **pending** until you run it and record the result. The Mac-only step 0.8 has its class-B evidence (G0.5 recovery report row 10) from an agent's run of the drop-in on 19 October 2026 UTC (20 October on the build Mac's clock), when the Mac's screen was found unlocked, and the recovery code it reads has not changed since; on 22 October 2026 the drop-in gained steps 13-15, for the lock harness, and the whole drop-in, those steps included, passed from clean in an agent's run on `9cdb0a02` with the screen unlocked, at 22:36-22:44 UTC that day (23 October on the build Mac's clock), so 0.8 asks you to run it again only if what it builds changes after that commit (until 22 October 2026, after the review of the decisions round, this line called 0.8 done for the current code: its findings HON-6 and CHK-07; and until 23 October 2026 it said that no run of the whole drop-in had passed since that day's change, and asked you to run it once more).
- **Your decisions of 22 October 2026 are in it** (the M0 decision page; `M0-gate.md` Sections 3 and 4). Device A, the oldest supported iPhone, is your iPhone 11 Pro (D-1 (b)), and the iPad's steps stay in M0 (D-1 (c)); you have device A, so the exit waits for Session 8 (D-2 (a)); your keys and a local model server run G0.5 AI steps 5-7 in M0 (D-4 (a)); corrections C47, C49, C50 and C56 are accepted, so the steps they move wait for M6, M5, M3 and M2 (D-6); App Transport Security needs no run at the minimum OS (D-9 (b)); and every step Section 7 marks blocking passes before the M0 exit (D-10 (i)). G0.5 recovery step 3 runs with the lock harness, the app "G05 Lock" (`Spikes/G05-Recovery/LockHarness`), built in Stage 0 before the sessions (PO-23 (b); 6.1). You sign the security, design, QA and technical-lead reviews of Session 10 yourself, each only after the sessions that produce its evidence. **Your answers of 23 October 2026** (`M0-gate.md` Section 1, item 4) are in it too: H-1's exception also covers pull request #5 and the merge of `m0-decisions` into `main` before the M0 exit, and no other merge: agent work goes into `m0-decisions`, and bringing it to `main` before the exit is a further merge your answer did not cover (run 1, 0.3); you have an iPad with a keyboard and a trackpad or mouse for Session 9, whose iPadOS you check before Session 0 and whose model and build 0.4 records, so whether it can run Session 9 is not verified until then (Section 1.1); and you confirmed the readings of D-6, D-7 and D-9 (b) (Section 6). A decision is not a device result: every step stays pending until it runs.
- **Each step gives its actions, expected result and record.** Where a step uses the checklist's own material instead, it says so and names it: G0.4 step 1.3's result tables and G0.5 AI step 9.2's strings (their expected values), G0.4 step 1.2's pull loop (1.4), the Python check of G0.4 step 3.4 (3.2), and the entries G0.3 step 24 lists (5.6). (Until 20 October 2026 this line named only the first two.)
- **The corrections.** Section 8 lists 56 corrections this runbook made to the checklists, reports and ADRs as they stood at `471b143`, each with the exact text to change. M0 assembly applied them to those files, except the ones that wait for a decision (C28, C46's script change, C47, C49, C50, C51 and C56): where one of those differs from its checklist, this runbook's version holds until its decision is taken, and Section 7 says what each changes. On 22 October 2026 your decision D-6 accepted C47, C49, C50 and C56, and C28 with C50: they are applied in this runbook, in the checklists and reports of this folder (Section 8's first paragraph) and in the ADRs they name (ADR-0009, ADR-0011, ADR-0013 and ADR-0010); D-1 (c) leaves C51 unapplied; C46's script change still waits for the G0.2/M6 owner.
- **Which steps block the M0 gate** and which can wait for a later milestone, with the reason for each: Section 7.
- **Who wrote it.** Agents, from the checklists, reports and harness code (14 October 2026, at M0 integration), with changes on 19 and 20 October 2026, on 22 October 2026 to record your decisions and then to answer the review of that record (integration log section 18), and on 23 October 2026 to answer a review of that answer (section 19), and then to record your answers of that day and answer a review of the second answer (section 20), and to answer a review of that record, its fidelity findings (section 21), its honesty findings (section 22) and its owner-readability findings (section 23), and then a review of those three fixes (section 24), and to record the agent's run of the whole G0.5 recovery drop-in with the screen unlocked (0.8; section 25), and then to answer a review of that record (section 26), and to record, in this line only, the review of that answer (section 27), and on 24 and 26 October 2026, at the M0 exit preparation, to add dated notes on what that preparation did (sections 28-30). Agent-reviewed as merged; the changes since were read by later agent reviews, the last seven of the records of your decisions at `88d4e1c7`, of their first fix at `d0a24c43`, of the second at `a1ea66d2`, of the follow-up at `19489f10`, of its three fixes at `660c2b3a` and of the record of the drop-in's run at `91c3b46a` and its fix at `26530d49`, each of which found more to fix, so none accepted them; the follow-up's fourth fix of 23 October 2026, and the second fix of the drop-in's record, have not been reviewed, nor have the notes of the M0 exit preparation; human review pending. (Until 23 October 2026 this line said the changes since the merge had not been reviewed again: the review of the decisions round's fix, its finding HON-12; and until the fix of the review of the drop-in's record, the same day, it said that the record had not been reviewed; and until that record's second fix, the same day, that the record's fix had not been reviewed.) The agents built the harnesses for a generic iOS device and checked test filters on their own simulator (Section 9).
- **The runbook item's review record.** Every author, review and fix round of `m0-int-runbook`, the item that wrote this runbook at M0 integration, handed over on 24 October 2026 from the orchestrating session's workflow journals (agent reviews; human sign-off pending): its final review, round 3, accepted `6f339473` with four minor findings, R3-01 to R3-04, left open at the merge and not written up here: [`evidence/M0-Integration/item-reviews/runbook.md`](evidence/M0-Integration/item-reviews/runbook.md).

**OS versions.** Every step runs on the iOS or iPadOS the device has, 26.x or 27.x; record the exact version and build. A device on 27.x (the iPad, if it has iPadOS 27, or device A if Software Update offers only iOS 27) is used as it is, since a device cannot be put back to an earlier major version: every harness targets 26.0 and runs on later versions, and a result taken on 27.x is a result on that build, never one on 26.x. Xcode 27.1, the pin, brings the iOS 27.1 SDK: if it cannot install a harness on a device whose OS is newer than that, record the step `blocked` with Xcode's message and ask an agent what to do, rather than updating Xcode, which would leave the pin (D-3). Do not update phone B, device A or the iPad during M0, once Session 0.4 has recorded each one's build: results are recorded per OS build, so Session 0.4 turns off each device's automatic OS updates. (Until 23 October 2026 this paragraph named 26.x only and no step turned automatic updates off: the review of the decisions round's fix, its finding OWN2-06.) One step asks for the minimum supported OS, iOS 26.0: G0.5 AI step 1 (App Transport Security; report row D1, plan 14.3). No device on hand runs 26.0, so the step runs on phone B (Session 1.3) and again on device A if A has an earlier 26.x (Session 8.8), and your decision D-9 says whether that counts as the minimum OS (correction C48). You decided D-9 (b) on 22 October 2026: ADR-0011 records an exception instead, Sylvia's own `EndpointPolicy` being the cleartext gate on every OS version, so phone B's run stands without a claim about iOS 26.0, and 8.8 on A is a record no decision needs.

### 1.2 Accessories

- **A cable for each device** (USB-C for phone B; Lightning for device A, the iPhone 11 Pro; USB-C for most iPads). Finder file sharing needs it (G0.3 steps 19-23), and it is much faster than Wi-Fi for installing G0.4's 9.3 GB test bundle (Session 1). A Mac's port also charges the phone, so the Low Power Mode runs of Session 1 are made over Wi-Fi, without it (correction C40).
- **A Bluetooth headset or headphones** with a play/pause control: G0.4 steps 4, 6 and 7.3, which block M0 (wired headphones also do for steps 4 and 7.3, with an adapter for USB-C or Lightning; step 6 needs Bluetooth).
- **A charger** for the long automated runs (Session 1).
- **A hardware keyboard** (Bluetooth, or an iPad keyboard) for G0.6 step 17 on phone B (Session 7.13, with the last press of G0.6 step 12; it blocks M0: the G0.6 report's hardware keyboard row is pending-device), and steps 17 and 28 on the iPad (Session 9); step 17 on device A moved to M3 with correction C50, which you accepted on 22 October 2026.
- **A trackpad or mouse for the iPad** (a Magic Keyboard with trackpad, or a Bluetooth mouse or trackpad) for the pointer part of G0.1 PDF step 8 (Session 9.7; ADR-0005's pending device list names it; added to the checklist by the gate's review round 4).

### 1.3 Networks, accounts and apps

- **One Wi-Fi network shared by the Mac and the phone**, on which they can reach each other (G0.2, G0.5 AI step 2). A network that isolates its clients (some guest networks do) fails G0.2 steps 2-5 for reasons that have nothing to do with Sylvia.
- **A second network: cellular data on phone B, and Tailscale** installed and logged in to the same tailnet on phone B and the Mac (G0.2 step 6).
- **iCloud Drive** on the Mac and phone B, signed in to the same Apple Account, with about 5 GB of free iCloud storage for the files of G0.3 steps 6, 7, 12 and 18 (4.8 GB, nearly all of it the 4.5 GiB placeholder of step 18). Start the upload in Session 0; it can take an hour or more.
- **A Mail account** on phone B you can send yourself an attachment with (G0.3 step 9); **Safari** with internet access (G0.3 step 8); **Books** with a PDF you added yourself, and one other reading app (G0.3 step 10).
- **Siri** on, and allowed when the phone is locked (G0.4 step 10).
- **The team's Apple Developer account** (A4KBDX665Z): the account holder accepts the latest agreement and adds the account to Xcode in Session 0.
- **People:** none to book. Since your instruction of 22 October 2026 you sign the security, design, QA and technical-lead reviews of Session 10 yourself, and you judge the design steps of Sessions 2 (2.7 and 2.8, G0.1 EPUB), 7, 8 and 9 (G0.6 on phone B, device A and the iPad; until 23 October 2026 this item said Sessions 7 and 8, leaving out the iPad's, which your decision D-1 (c) keeps in M0: the review of the decisions round's fix, its findings FID2-04 and OWN2-05); each sign-off is recorded only once the sessions that produce its evidence have run. Every review so far was an agent's, not an independent person's: before any public release, have an outside security reviewer look at the security-relevant design and code (Section 6). (Until 22 October 2026 this item asked you to book a security reviewer and a designer early.)
- API keys and a local model server: your decision D-4 (a) of 22 October 2026 runs G0.5 AI steps 5-7 in M0 with them (Section 1.6; about 1.5 h and a few cents of API use).

### 1.5 Signing and installing the harnesses (team A4KBDX665Z, automatic signing)

**What the build Mac has today** (read-only checks on 14 October 2026; [`build-mac-state.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/build-mac-state.txt)):

- one signing identity, "Apple Development: JUSTIN EVIDON";
- one profile that can sign Sylvia's harnesses, "iOS Team Provisioning Profile: *" (any app ID of team A4KBDX665Z, expires 17 August 2027), listing exactly **one** registered device (which one was not checked: the check prints no device identifier);
- **no developer account in Xcode** (Xcode > Settings > Accounts is empty), so Xcode cannot register a new device, create an App Group or make a Mac development profile;
- every fixture the device steps need, already in `~/Library/Caches/SylviaFixtures`.

**What that means.** Every harness's device build command was built for a generic iOS device on this Mac (build only; no device was used): [`device-builds/summary.tsv`](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/summary.tsv), Section 9.

- G0.1 EPUB and PDF, the G0.2 harness and its ATS probe app, G0.4, the G0.5 AI probe apps and the G0.6 design catalog build and sign with what is on the Mac, so they can be installed on the one device the profile lists; any other device must be registered first (below).
- **G0.3** uses the App Group `group.com.jevidon.sylvia.spike`. Its signed build stops with "Provisioning profile "iOS Team Provisioning Profile: *" doesn't include the App Groups capability" ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g03-intake-tests-signed.log)); the same build compiles with signing turned off ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g03-intake-tests-unsigned.log)).
- **The Mac identity probe** (G0.2 step 20) stops with "No profiles for 'com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.identityprobe' were found" ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g02-mac-identity-probe-signing.log)).
- **G0.5 recovery** is a Swift package: its test bundle builds, but `build-for-testing` gives a package's test bundle no signing step ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g05-recovery-tests.log)). Whether `xcodebuild test` signs it for the phone is only known when Session 1 runs it. Its lock harness (an app, since 22 October 2026; 6.1) builds for a generic iOS device and signs with what is on the Mac ([record](evidence/M0-Decisions/harness/harness-checks-clean/harness-device-signing.txt)).
- A device the team has not registered (A and C, and B if it is not the one the profile lists) cannot run anything until it is registered.

**Do once, before Session 1** (Session 0, steps 0.1-0.4): accept the agreement, add the account to Xcode, connect each device and check that the team has registered it. Every device command below passes two flags (correction C45). `-allowProvisioningUpdates` lets xcodebuild use the account to create and update profiles, App IDs and certificates, G0.3's App Group included; it does **not** register a device. `-allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration` registers the destination device on the developer portal if it is not registered, and takes effect only together with the first (`xcodebuild -help` of Xcode 27.0: [`tool-help.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/tool-help.txt); the same in 27.1, the pinned Xcode since 20 October 2026: [`tool-help-compare.log`](evidence/M0-Integration/repin-27.1/tool-help-compare.log)). Without it, a build for an unregistered device stops at signing. Since M0 assembly applied C45, seven of the eight checklists pass it in their device commands too (the G0.6 checklist installs from Xcode's Run, with automatic signing), but the command of G0.2 step 23 does not (4.19 adds it), nor does the G0.2 probe script of Session 0.7, which therefore registers the Mac first (C46). (Until 20 October 2026 this paragraph said that no checklist passed it, as at `471b143`, and 4.19 left it out.) The checks in Section 9 left both flags out on purpose: they contact Apple's developer service, which the agent may not do, so registration itself was not tried here.

**The device identifier.** The checklists take `<device-id>` from `xcrun devicectl list devices`. In Xcode 27.0, and in 27.1 by the same help text, that command's Identifier column shows the device's UDID, falling back to its ECID and then to its CoreDevice identifier, and says which kind it shows; devicectl's `--device` accepts any of them, but `xcodebuild -destination id=` needs the UDID (`devicectl list devices --help` and the `--device` options in [`tool-help.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/tool-help.txt)). So use the Identifier when devicectl gives the UDID (copy the identifier alone); otherwise take the UDID from Xcode > Window > Devices and Simulators > the device > Identifier. Below, `$DEVICE_ID` is that UDID (correction C1; this runbook first said the column is CoreDevice's own identifier, which the tool's help contradicts).

**Timings.** Plan 19.1: device timings come from Release builds without a debugger. The timed steps use either an app built in Release with `xcodebuild`, installed with `xcrun devicectl device install app` and launched from the Home Screen (G0.1 open times, the G0.4 harness, the G0.2 harness on device A in 8.7), or `xcodebuild test -configuration Release` (G0.4 step 1.5, G0.5 recovery step 2), which attaches no debugger; the Debug runs of the same tests are recorded beside them with their configuration. In 8.7 Instruments, a profiler and not a debugger, is attached to the Release app for its memory and hangs, and the record says so. Xcode's Run (Debug, debugger attached) is fine for the other steps.

### 1.6 API keys and a local model server (G0.5 AI steps 5-7 in M0, decision D-4 (a); step 8 in M5)

These steps run `swift test` on the Mac against real services, not on the phone. They are what the G0.5 gate's "demonstrate each provider dialect plus a local endpoint ... a transcription endpoint" needs (report rows P1, P3 and P4; Section 7). Your decision D-4 (a) of 22 October 2026 keeps them in M0 (Section 6).

- **Keys go in environment variables only, typed at a hidden prompt, for one command, then removed:** `read -rs OPENAI_API_KEY && export OPENAI_API_KEY`, run the test, `unset OPENAI_API_KEY` (G0.5 AI checklist step 6 shows each one). Never put a key in a file, a shell profile, a command line (it would land in shell history), a screenshot or a record: records name the model, never the key. Use a fresh terminal and a key you can revoke afterwards.
- **A local model server** (step 5): for example LM Studio's server on a Mac on the same network, bound to that Mac's LAN address, with a small text model loaded; note its base URL (`http://<lan-ip>:1234/v1`) and model id.
- **A transcription endpoint** (step 7): OpenAI's `/audio/transcriptions`, or a local Whisper server.
- **A gateway** (step 8, Session 6.7; M5's since 22 October 2026, when you accepted correction C49 with decisions D-4 (d) and D-6, so not needed for M0: Section 7): Hermes or another OpenAI-compatible gateway whose operator confirms a text-only route with no tools.
- Checked on this Mac: without `SYLVIA_LIVE_PROVIDER_TESTS=1`, `swift test --filter LiveProviderTests` builds and all four live tests skip with their reasons, so nothing is sent ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g05-ai-live-tests-skip.log)).

### 1.7 Sound

The automated tests and the G0.4 harness play at 1 % volume unless asked otherwise (G0.4 Setup 4), so measurements in G0.4 step 1 are taken at that test volume; its result tables say so in their environment lines. Steps that say **listen** use the harness's full-volume toggle (G0.4 step 2.4).

## 2. How to record results

For every step write: the date (UTC), the tester, the device model and identifier (for example iPhone18,1), its OS version and build, the commit you built from (`git rev-parse HEAD`), the build configuration (Debug or Release), the result and what you saw; for timing and transfer steps also the free storage (Settings > General > iPhone Storage) and whether the phone was charging or in Low Power Mode (plan 19.1 asks for the device and storage conditions); then attach the logs and screenshots the step names.

- **Results** (the same words in every gate, where the checklists use four different ones: correction C31): `passed-device` (the step's expected result, on that device); `failed` (anything else: write what happened instead, and never edit an expected result to match); `inconclusive` (only where a step allows it, such as G0.5 recovery step 3); `blocked` (a device, account or person is missing: say which). An inconclusive or blocked step leaves its report row pending-device or pending-human, with the reason.
- **Privacy:** never record a pairing code, token, invitation secret or API key, an IP address beyond its first two octets (in every gate, as G0.5 AI's checklist asks: `192.168.x.x`, `100.101.x.x`), the tailnet part of a MagicDNS name (`mac.[tailnet].ts.net`), personal data, or file names of your own files; redact (`[pairing code redacted]`) or crop. Session 4 gives the command that redacts addresses in a log. Screenshots as PNG under 2 MB; logs as plain text.
- **Then:** in the gate's report, change each row the step feeds from pending-device or pending-human to `passed-device` or `failed`, naming the device, OS build and evidence file (or leave that to an agent working from your records), and add one row per sitting to [`evidence/M0-runbook/sessions.md`](evidence/M0-runbook/sessions.md). After each sitting, commit its records and evidence on `m0-decisions` (0.3, step 6), before you ask an agent to work from them: every agent starts from `m0-decisions`.

| Gate | Records go in | Files (as each checklist names them) |
| --- | --- | --- |
| G0.1 EPUB | `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G01-EPUB/device/` | `step-NN.md` per step, with PNG or MP4 under 2 MB; step 11 adds `device-tests.log` and the files pulled from the app |
| G0.1 PDF | `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G01-PDF/` | `device-run.md` for all steps, and `device-<step>.png` |
| G0.2 | `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G02-Transfer/device/` | `<NN>-<short-name>.md`, with the logs it names: `<NN>-events.jsonl`, `<NN>-imports.json`, and the request logs of Session 4's logging source, written with the codes already redacted: `08-source.log`, `09-source.log` and `09-source-2.log`, `13a-source.log`, `13b-source.log`, `14-16-source.log`, `21-source.log` and `22-source.log` |
| G0.3 | `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G03-Intake/` | `device-<model>-<date>.md`, screenshots beside it |
| G0.4 | `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G04-Audio/device/` | `step-NN-<what>.log`, the tables in `step-01/` and `step-01-release/`, PNG |

Record: session log, with the Xcode version. 5 min.

**0.3 Commit** (before 0.2; its steps 1-6 are run 1's, `M0-gate.md` Section 1, item 1, under the same numbers). Do, in the repository on the build Mac:

Not `m0-integrate`, not `main` at `4c73da57` (pull request #5), and not a `main` you have not brought up to date: none has the lock harness of 6.1, nor the runbook and checklists as your decisions left them. 2. Before you ask an agent for anything: correct `CLAUDE.md`'s milestone paragraph, which still says the M0 work is combined on `m0-integrate`, here on `m0-decisions` (`M0-gate.md` Section 7, item 5, gives a wording), and commit it, or ask an agent to make that commit on `m0-decisions`. Every agent you ask starts from `m0-decisions`, so the change must be on it, and made before step 3, so that a merge into `main` carries it. *(Done on 24 October 2026, at your request, by the orchestrating session: `5aec2518`, on top of `main` just after pull request #6, not on `m0-decisions`, so the merge did not carry it; it is not yet on `main` itself: `M0-gate.md` Section 1, item 1.)*
3. Only if you want to build from `main`: merge `m0-decisions` into it by pull request now, a merge H-1's exception covers since your answer of 23 October 2026 (run 1), after step 2 and before any agent work is in `m0-decisions` (step 5); then `git checkout main`, then `git pull --ff-only origin main`, since your checkout's own `main` stays where it was until you fetch it (on 23 October 2026 this repository's local `main` was `b51d19ef`, 163 commits behind the `origin/main` it last fetched, `4c73da57`, and its tree held only `Docs/`: `evidence/M0-Decisions/fix-2/gate-checks.log`, part 1). Otherwise stay on `m0-decisions`. *(Done: pull request #6, merge commit `96bdc7e9`, on 23 October 2026 UTC, 24 October on the build Mac's clock; this repository's `main` is at it.)*
4. Check that `Spikes/G05-Recovery/LockHarness/project.yml` exists (for example `ls Spikes/G05-Recovery/LockHarness`), and note `git rev-parse HEAD` in the session log. Build every session from that commit; if you move to another, note it in the session log (correction C30).
5. **Agent work.** Agent work you start during the sessions (the R4-01 and R4-02 fix of run 1, or a fix after the review `M0-gate.md` Section 7, item 5 recommends) lands on a branch of its own, made from `m0-decisions`, which your request names; *(24 October 2026: the R4-01 and R4-02 fix is done, by an agent, before the sessions, on a branch of its own made from `main` after pull request #6, with `CLAUDE.md`'s correction, not from `m0-decisions`, and assembled on `m0-exit-prep`; reviewed next: ADR-0014 status and section 4)* it never changes the commit you have checked out, and it reaches your build only when you merge it into `m0-decisions` (`git checkout m0-decisions`, then `git merge <its branch>`), between sessions, noting the new `git rev-parse HEAD` in the session log. Start an agent whose work changes the same records as another's (both of those fixes can edit `M0-gate.md` and ADR-0014) only once the other's branch is merged into `m0-decisions`, so that it starts from that work and neither undoes the other. The same holds between your own edits of the records (the session log, a report row, an ADR's Deciders line) and an agent's: commit yours on `m0-decisions` (step 6) before you start an agent whose work changes the same records, and leave those records to it until you have merged its branch. Never merge it into `main`, or open a pull request of it into `main`, before the M0 exit: your answer of 23 October 2026 extends H-1's exception to pull request #5 and to the merge of `m0-decisions` into `main`, not to other merges (ADR-0002, Branches). Bringing that work to `main` before the exit is a further merge that your answer did not cover: a second merge of `m0-decisions` it does not name, and a first one made after the work is in `m0-decisions` it did not say it covers, since the question you answered described `m0-decisions` as the records of your decisions, the lock harness and their fixes. So either say first whether your exception covers such a merge (ADR-0002 records your answer: its open item), or build from `m0-decisions` until the exit: if you build from `main`, take the work by moving your build to `m0-decisions` (`git checkout m0-decisions`), noting the new commit.
6. **Your session records, on `m0-decisions`.** After each sitting, commit what it produced on `m0-decisions`: the session log's row, the evidence its steps wrote (Section 2's table names the folders) and the report rows you changed. If you built from `main`, first `git checkout m0-decisions`: your uncommitted files go with you, and `m0-decisions` then holds your records while `main` does not, so build the next sessions from `m0-decisions`, whose code is your build's until agent work is merged into it (step 5). Then `git add` those files and folders and `git commit`. Do it before you ask an agent to work from them: every agent starts from `m0-decisions`, and sees nothing uncommitted or on `main`. A commit of records alone changes nothing a step builds (step 7): note the commit the next sitting builds from in its row, as step 4 says. Nothing reaches `main` before the M0 exit except the merge of step 3: your records, like agent work, reach it after the exit, unless you first say that your exception covers bringing them there (step 5).
7. **When a session already run is repeated:** only when the commit you move to changes something a step built, copied or ran, or the step's action, expected result, criterion, or pass, fail or inconclusive rule in its checklist; then repeat only those steps, on the new commit, recording each run with its commit, the later result counting. What a step builds, copies or runs is more than its harness: the design catalog of Sessions 7-9 links `Packages/Design`, the EPUB harness bundles `Fixtures/epub` and the design package's Literata fonts, the G0.4 harness bundles `Fixtures/audio` and the fixture cache that `Scripts/fixtures` fills, 0.8's drop-in reads `Contracts/`, and every session sources `Scripts/lib`; so a token value changed in `Packages/Design` after Session 7, as G0.6's row on adjusting token values expects, repeats the G0.6 steps that show that token, on each device they ran on. Where only a rule changed and the logs you kept hold everything the new rule reads, judge those logs again instead of repeating the step, and say so in the session log. A change of wording only, or of another step, needs no repeat. To see what changed: `git diff --stat <the commit in the session log> HEAD -- Apps Packages Spikes Fixtures Scripts Contracts Docs/Stage0/device-checklists`.
8. Which sessions can start before that review: `M0-gate.md` Section 1, item 2 (every step except 0.8 and the lock harness's runs, 6.1 and its repeat on device A, builds code that no later round touched; Session 6 after the review, if you have it made).

(This step's history: `M0-gate.md` Section 6.3, items 74 and 85.) 2 min, about 10 min more for step 2, and a few minutes after each sitting for step 6.

**0.4 Devices.** Do, for each of the three devices you will use (phone B; device A, your iPhone 11 Pro; and the iPad C, which you have, with its keyboard and trackpad or mouse: your answer of 23 October 2026; its model is first recorded here): connect it by cable, unlock it and tap Trust; open Xcode > Window > Devices and Simulators and select it; if Developer Mode is off (phone B's is on, ADR-0001), turn on Settings > Privacy & Security > Developer Mode, which iOS shows once the device has been connected to Xcode, let it restart and confirm; wait until Xcode has prepared the device, and copy its Identifier (the UDID). Then check its registration: at developer.apple.com > Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles > Devices, look for that UDID. If it is not listed, register it there (+, a name and the UDID), or leave it to the first device command, whose `-allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration` registers it (Section 1.5); each registration uses one of the team's yearly device slots. Then, on the device, turn off its automatic OS updates, so the build you record stays the one every later result is taken on (Section 1.1): Settings > General > Software Update > Automatic Updates, with the automatic download and install of iOS (or iPadOS) updates off (these names were not checked on a device). Leave security responses on; if one installs during M0, record the device's new build in the session log, the results before it staying recorded under the build they ran on. Expect: every device registered before its first install, and its automatic updates off. Record in the session log: model, model identifier (`xcrun devicectl device info details --device $DEVICE_ID` prints it as the product type), OS version and build, whether the device was already registered, and that its automatic updates are off. For the iPad, that record is what shows whether it can run Session 9 (iPadOS 26 or later): run 1 has its iPadOS checked as device A is (Section 1.1), and until that check and that record whether it can run Session 9 is not verified. 10 min per device. (Until 23 October 2026 no step turned the devices' automatic updates off, while Section 1.1 asked you to keep their OS builds: the review of the decisions round's fix, its finding OWN2-06. The sentence on the iPad's record was added the same day, after the review of the follow-up, its finding HON4-03.)

The probe needs a Mac development profile, and that profile needs this Mac registered with the team. The probe script passes `-allowProvisioningUpdates` but not `-allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration`, so it cannot register the Mac (Section 1.5). After 0.1 and 0.2, first build the probe once with both flags:

```sh
  -destination platform=macOS -derivedDataPath Spikes/G02-Transfer/MacIdentityProbe/.build/DerivedData \

```

Expect: `** BUILD SUCCEEDED **`, and this Mac listed at developer.apple.com > Certificates, Identifiers & Profiles > Devices. (Not tried here: it needs the account. Without either flag the same build stops with "No profiles for 'com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.identityprobe' were found": [log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g02-mac-identity-probe-signing.log).) If it still stops at signing, register the Mac by hand on that Devices page (+, platform macOS, a name and the Mac's Provisioning UDID, which System Information shows under Hardware; this Mac has that field: [`build-mac-state.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/build-mac-state.txt)), then run the build again. Then:

```sh
Spikes/G02-Transfer/Scripts/mac-identity-probe-run.sh Docs/Stage0/evidence/G02-Transfer/keychain-identity-signed.log
```

Expect: `RESULT: PASS`, with `G02-PROBE backend: data-protection keychain`, a `kSecAttrAccessible` value equal to the one printed for `kSecAttrAccessibleAfterFirstUnlockThisDeviceOnly` on the same line, `kSecAttrSynchronizable: false`, an access group ending in `com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.identityprobe`, and no item in the login keychain. Record: commit the regenerated log on `m0-decisions` (0.3, step 6); update the G0.2 row. 20 min.

**0.8 G0.5 recovery report row 10: the class-B run on the Mac** (correction C27: the report asks for it in row 10 and open question 7, the checklist has no step). The host's class-B tests skip while the Mac's screen is locked, and the committed clean run was made with the screen locked. *Its class-B evidence stands for the current recovery code (19 October 2026 UTC, 20 October on the build Mac's clock):* M0 assembly's re-run of the drop-in, with the screen unlocked, read class B back on 53 files and directories (`evidence/M0-Integration/ci-71-g05-recovery-unlocked-dropin.log`, at `6371c88`); the M0 final fix then changed the protector's directory and protect paths (SEC-06), and the drop-in's runs in the fix's two complete runs had the screen locked. The second round's run of the drop-in on that code, from clean, found the screen unlocked (no agent unlocks it), and gave this step's expected result: "row 10 evidence present", class B read back on 53 files and directories, and the SQLite line (`evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/ci-71-g05-recovery-locked.log`, named, like the script that wrote it, `ci-71-allow-missing-class-b.sh`, for the locked screen of the earlier runs: this run found the screen unlocked and never used the relaxed guard, [`final-fix-2/README.md`](evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/README.md); `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g05-recovery/host-tests.log`). *The whole drop-in, with the lock harness's steps, passed on `9cdb0a02` (22 October 2026, 22:36-22:44 UTC; 23 October, 11:36-11:44, on the build Mac's clock):* on 22 October 2026 the drop-in gained steps 13-15, for the lock harness of 6.1: its lock against the package's, its unit tests and a UI smoke test of the app on the simulator, and its build for a phone. Every agent run of the whole drop-in that day found the Mac's screen locked and stopped at its guard, the check after its host tests that stops the run when their class-B lines are missing (`M0-gate.md` Section 2.10), until a waiter of the orchestrating session, not you, ran it from clean on `9cdb0a02` with the screen unlocked at its start and its end: every step passed, 13-15 included, with this step's expected lines (the row-10 line, class B read back on 53 files and directories, the SQLite line, and the lock harness's and the drop-in's closing lines), its wrapper's `ci.sh exit: 0` standing for the last line below (`evidence/M0-Decisions/assembly/unlocked-run-1/console.log`, its step logs in `evidence/M0-Decisions/assembly/unlocked-run-1/logs/`). The recovery package it reads for row 10 has not changed, so row 10's evidence stands from both runs. **So 0.8 needs nothing from you now. Run it again, as below, only if a commit you build from changes what the drop-in builds, copies or runs after `9cdb0a02`**: the recovery package or its lock harness (`Spikes/G05-Recovery`), the drop-in, `Scripts/lib` or `Contracts/` (0.3, step 7, gives the command that lists such changes); then before Session 6, and again whenever that code changes before the M0 exit. (Until 20 October 2026 this step said it was needed for the current code: M0 final review round 2, R6-04; and then, until the review of the decisions round on 22 October 2026, that it was done for the current code, although the drop-in had changed that day: its findings HON-6 and CHK-07; and until 23 October 2026 it asked you to run it once more, now, before Session 6, as the first run of the whole drop-in on the current code: `M0-gate.md` Section 6.3, item 92.) The run writes to a folder of its own: the report's documented evidence command (`G05_LOG_DIR=Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/logs`) would replace the logs its rows cite, and the drop-in's default folder, `.build/g05-recovery/logs`, is outside the evidence folder. Do, in zsh (the macOS default shell), and keep the screen unlocked until it ends (`caffeinate -d` keeps the display awake):

```sh
mkdir -p Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/row10-unlocked
G05_LOG_DIR=Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/row10-unlocked G05_SIM_NAME=Sylvia-M0-owner-recovery \
  caffeinate -d -i Scripts/ci.d/71-g05-recovery.sh 2>&1 | tee Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/row10-unlocked/console.log; \
  echo "drop-in exit ${pipestatus[1]}"
```

Expect: the last line reads `drop-in exit 0` (after a pipe, `$?` is the status of `tee` and reads 0 even when the drop-in failed; `${pipestatus[1]}` is the drop-in's, which `caffeinate` passes on, as checked in [`tool-help.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/tool-help.txt); in bash it is `${PIPESTATUS[0]}`), and the output has the line `row 10 evidence present: class-B readback and inheritance lines found in host-tests.log`; `row10-unlocked/host-tests.log` then has a `class B (non-default) requested: read back on 53 files and directories: ["NSFileProtectionCompleteUnlessOpen": 53]` line (every item class B; 53, not the 52 of `471b143`, since the gate's later review rounds, as the checklist's step 1.3 says; the assembly's re-run read 53, `evidence/M0-Integration/ci-71-g05-recovery-unlocked-dropin.log`), and a `files SQLite created in a class-B directory:` line; near the end come the lock harness's `G0.5 recovery lock harness: steps 13-15 passed` and the drop-in's `G0.5 recovery: all steps passed`. Record: the folder, which also holds the lock harness's logs (`harness-*`), committed on `m0-decisions` (0.3, step 6); update row 10 to cite it, and have the records of the whole drop-in's run, which cite the run of `9cdb0a02` (`M0-gate.md` Section 2.10), say that it ran again, on your commit (an agent can do it from the folder once it is committed). The drop-in creates and shuts down the simulator it names; delete it afterwards if you like (`xcrun simctl delete Sylvia-M0-owner-recovery`). About 10-15 min (the committed clean run took 6 minutes, `evidence/G05-Recovery/ci-clean-run.log`, and the lock harness's steps took 1-2 minutes in the agents' runs).

### Session 1 · Automated suites on phone B (about 2.5 h of your time, 6-8 h elapsed)

Phone B connected by cable (which also charges it), unlocked, Settings > Display & Brightness > Auto-Lock > Never, not in Low Power Mode unless a step says so. Run one `xcodebuild test` at a time. Steps 1.1-1.3 take about an hour with you there; the cable runs of 1.4 are long and need you only between them, so start them last (in the evening, for example). The Low Power Mode runs need you beside the phone for their whole length (below).

**Low Power Mode runs** (1.2 and 1.4; correction C40) are made on battery, so without the cable: a Mac's port charges the phone, and iOS turns Low Power Mode off once the battery reaches 80 % while charging ([Apple Support](https://support.apple.com/en-us/101604)). For each: charge the phone fully, unplug the cable, keep the phone on the Mac's Wi-Fi and turn on Settings > Battery > Low Power Mode; check that `xcrun devicectl list devices` still lists phone B as available (xcodebuild then reaches it over the network, which ADR-0001's pairing over the local network allows); if it is not available, wake the phone and wait for Xcode's Devices window to show it, and if it stays unavailable record the run as blocked. Then run the command. Installing over Wi-Fi is slower than by cable, much slower for G0.4's 9.3 GB test bundle. After the run, check that Low Power Mode is still on (yellow battery icon) and record the battery level at the start and the end.

**Keeping the phone unlocked in Low Power Mode** (correction C54). Low Power Mode "Sets Auto-Lock to 30 seconds" (the same Apple Support page), so the Auto-Lock > Never of the cable runs does not hold: the phone locks 30 s after your last touch, and a test run that needs it unlocked (the class-A checks of 1.2, the test host of 1.4) may then fail for that reason alone. So stay with the phone from the moment you start the command until it ends, and touch the clock in the status bar at least every 20 s (if the screen dims, touch it at once): a tap there presses no button in the test runner or the G0.4 harness, at most it scrolls a list to its top. If the phone locks anyway, note the UTC time; a run that locked part way is not a result: record it with that time and run it again. Record in each Low Power Mode run's notes that you kept the phone awake by touch. This is why G0.4's Low Power Mode run is its budget suite alone (1.4), about 10-30 min, not the full suite of over an hour.

```sh
sylvia_generate Spikes/G02-Transfer/iOS

xcodebuild test -project Spikes/G02-Transfer/iOS/G02Transfer.xcodeproj -scheme G02TransferHarness \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G02-Transfer/iOS/.build/DerivedData \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z \

  | tee Docs/Stage0/evidence/G02-Transfer/device/01-keychain-class.log
```

Expect: `✔ Test run with 3 tests in 1 suite passed` and `** TEST SUCCEEDED **` (the filter selects exactly those three tests: [`only-testing-ids.log`](evidence/M0-runbook/only-testing-ids.log)); xcodebuild's own `Executed 0 tests, with 0 failures` line is normal for Swift Testing. Record: the last 20 lines in `01-keychain-class.md`. 10 min.

**1.2 The persistence suite on the device, and save latency** (G0.5 recovery steps 1 and 2).

```sh
mkdir -p Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/device
cd Spikes/G05-Recovery
xcodebuild test -scheme G05Recovery-Package -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath .build/DerivedData \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z 2>&1 \
  | tee ../../Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/device/step-1-tests.log
cd ../..
```

Expect (checklist step 1.2-1.4): `Test run with 68 tests in 11 suites passed` (the iOS count on the current code: 68 in M0 assembly's re-run and in the second round of the M0 final fix, `evidence/M0-Integration/ci-71-g05-recovery-unlocked-dropin.log` and `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix-2/g05-recovery/simulator-tests.log`; 58 when this runbook was written, 67 in the gate's review-round-4 run); exactly four skipped tests, each with its reason (the lock probe, the persistence probe, and the two contract-fixture tests that cannot read the Mac's `Contracts/` from the phone); the protection-mismatch and class-B tests run and pass (record whether the mismatch test's line says the class change was "applied and verified" or "refused"; either passes); `class C (chosen; also the platform default) read back on 53 files and directories: ["NSFileProtectionCompleteUntilFirstUserAuthentication": 53]`, with no `none reported` entry (directories report a class on the device); `class B (non-default) requested: read back on 53 files and directories: ["NSFileProtectionCompleteUnlessOpen": 53]`, any other class on any item being a failure (the test's message names the item); the class-B SQLite line, `files SQLite created in a class-B directory: probe.sqlite=NSFileProtectionCompleteUnlessOpen probe.sqlite-wal=NSFileProtectionCompleteUnlessOpen probe.sqlite-shm=NSFileProtectionCompleteUnlessOpen`; writer pragmas `journal_mode=wal synchronous=FULL fullfsync=1 checkpoint_fullfsync=1`. If xcodebuild stops at signing or installing (Section 1.5: a package's test bundle is not signed by a build), record the error verbatim as the result. Then step 2: run the same command twice more by cable, saving `step-2-run2.log` and `step-2-run3.log`, and once in Low Power Mode on battery over Wi-Fi (above; C40), saving `step-2-low-power.log`. Then the same measurement in a Release build (plan 19.1: budgets come from release builds; correction C41), the latency test alone, once by cable and once in Low Power Mode on battery over Wi-Fi:

```sh
cd Spikes/G05-Recovery
xcodebuild test -scheme G05Recovery-Package -configuration Release ENABLE_TESTABILITY=YES -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" \
  -derivedDataPath .build/DerivedData-release -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z \
  '-only-testing:SylviaRecoveryTests/DurabilityTests/latency()' 2>&1 \
  | tee ../../Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-Recovery/device/step-2-release.log
cd ../..
```

It builds for a generic iOS device on this Mac ([log](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g05-recovery-tests-release.log)), and the filter selects that one test, while the same filter without `()` selects none ([`only-testing-ids.log`](evidence/M0-runbook/only-testing-ids.log)). Save the Low Power Mode run as `step-2-release-low-power.log`. Expect: `✔ Test run with 1 test in 1 suite passed`, and in each log the line `capture save latency synchronous=FULL fullfsync=ON: n=400 p50=… p95=… p99=… max=…` with p95 at most 200 ms in all six runs, Debug and Release alike; a p95 above it is a failure, not something to retry away (a run that locked part way, above, is run again; it is not a retry of a result). Record, in `step-1-2-results.txt` beside the logs: from `step-1-tests.log`, the `SQLite …` line (version and compile options; a version other than the host's 3.51.0 is noted, not a failure), the `writer pragmas: …` line, the `files SQLite created in a class-A directory: …` line (an observation, not pass or fail: on the Mac the `-shm` file comes out class C; note whether the phone does the same), the `default GRDB connection: SQLITE_DBCONFIG_DEFENSIVE=… trusted_schema=…` line, and whether the mismatch test's line says "applied and verified" or "refused" (checklist steps 1.2-1.4; ADR-0003's open item asks for the SQLite version and the pragma readback on each device); then the six p95 values, each with its configuration, power state and battery levels. 15 min, then 3 × 10 min and 2 × 5 min.

**1.3 App Transport Security on the device** (G0.5 AI step 1, report row D1, on phone B's iOS 26.7; repeated on device A in Session 8.8 if A has an earlier 26.x, a record since your decision D-9 (b); correction C48. Its scheme also runs step 4's UI test.)

```sh
sylvia_generate Spikes/G05-AI/ATSProbe
mkdir -p Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-AI/device
cd Spikes/G05-AI/ATSProbe
xcodebuild test -project G05ATSProbe.xcodeproj -scheme G05ATSProbe -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" \
  -derivedDataPath .build/DerivedData -disableAutomaticPackageResolution \
  DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z CODE_SIGN_STYLE=Automatic CODE_SIGN_IDENTITY="Apple Development" -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration \
  2>&1 | tee ../../../Docs/Stage0/evidence/G05-AI/device/step-01-ats.log
cd ../../..
```

When iOS asks for Local Network access for a probe app ("ATS default", "ATS local", "ATS CIDR"), allow it and run the command again; note that the prompt appeared. Expect (checklist step 1.3): `plainHTTPToAPrivateLANAddress`, `plainHTTPToAPublicIPLiteral` and `plainHTTPToAQualifiedNameIsBlockedInEveryVariant` pass; on the device the default variant **blocks** cleartext to the private LAN address (URLError -1022) while the `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` and CIDR variants pass ATS and are refused locally (-1004); `plainHTTPToAnotherDeviceOnTheLAN` is skipped. If the default variant does not block the private address, record it: ADR-0011 must then say that Sylvia's own `EndpointPolicy` is the only cleartext gate; if the `NSAllowsLocalNetworking` variant lets the public IP literal through ATS (`ats-blocked=false`), record that too: shipping that key would then leave public cleartext to `EndpointPolicy` alone. The UI test `testLeavingTheForegroundMidStreamLeavesAnInterruptedPartialAnswer` and its `SUSPENSION-PROBE` line are G0.5 AI step 4's result. Record: in `step-01-ats.txt` the three `ATS-PROBE variant=… private-ip=…` lines, the three `public-ip=192.0.2.1 … ats-blocked=…` lines, the three `localhost=` lines and the final `✔` or `✘` summary, with the exact iOS version and build: row D1 asked for the minimum supported OS (iOS 26.0), which no device on hand runs; your decision D-9 (b) of 22 October 2026 records an exception in ADR-0011 instead, so this run is row D1's result, with no claim about iOS 26.0 (Section 6); the UI test lines in `step-04-suspension.txt` (G0.5 AI step 4, row D4). 20 min.


Record: the logs and table folders named above, under `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G04-Audio/device/`, and each step's result in the report's rows (Section 2). This step turns ADR-0006's provisional codec list into the release list. 1 h of your time (with the phone for the Low Power Mode run); 5-7 h elapsed for the three full runs, the budget run and the Release subset.

### Session 2 · Reading on phone B (about 5 h)

**G0.1 EPUB.** Install the Release harness (the checklist's command; [checked](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g01-epub-release.log)) and launch "Sylvia EPUB Spike" from the Home Screen. Record each step in `G01-EPUB/device/step-NN.md`.

```sh
mkdir -p Docs/Stage0/evidence/G01-EPUB/device
sylvia_generate Spikes/G01-EPUB
xcodebuild build -project Spikes/G01-EPUB/G01EPUB.xcodeproj -scheme EPUBHarness -configuration Release \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G01-EPUB/.build/DerivedData-device \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z
xcrun devicectl device install app --device "$DEVICE_ID" \
  Spikes/G01-EPUB/.build/DerivedData-device/Build/Products/Release-iphoneos/EPUBHarness.app
```

**2.1 Finger selection and the menu** (EPUB step 1; corrected, C2). Do: open `se-meditations.epub` and go to Book I; long-press **"From"** at the start of the paragraph "From my governor, to be neither of the green nor of the blue party…" (or long-press "governor" and drag the start handle back to "From"), then drag the end handle to "fights" (just before the semicolon); tap Highlight; tap Saved. Expect: the menu shows Highlight, Note, Look Up and Ask (system items such as Copy may appear too); the status line reads "Saved"; an ochre fill with a darker ochre underline on every line; the selection disappears; the Saved row reads "Quote: From my governor, … gladiators’ fights" with exactly the selected text, nothing cut off (the book prints a typographic apostrophe, U+2019, in "gladiators’"; the checklist's straight one is a typing difference, not a mismatch). Record: screenshots of the highlight and the Saved row. 10 min.

**2.2 Across a note reference** (EPUB step 2). Do: select from "From my mother," through "piety and beneficence" (it crosses the superscript 3); Note; type "test note"; Save. Expect: the alert shows the text with the "3"; "Saved"; the Saved list shows "Quote: From my mother,3 piety and beneficence" and "Your note: test note". Record whether the handles could be placed on both sides of the superscript without jumping. 5 min.

**2.3 Past a page edge** (EPUB step 3). Do: in Book I, start a selection in the last line of a page and drag the end handle onto the next page. Record what iOS allows, either way: whether the selection extends past the visible page; if it cannot, it stops at the page edge and the captured text must equal what was shown selected, never silently shorter (an observation for finding F8: it decides whether long quotations need a "continue selection" affordance). 5 min.

**2.4 Look Up and Ask** (EPUB step 4). Do: select "beneficence" and tap Look Up; then select a sentence and tap Ask. Expect: Look Up shows the system dictionary sheet; Ask shows an alert "Ask (not sent)" with the selected passage, and nothing is sent anywhere (the harness has no provider code). Record: screenshots of both in `step-04.md`. 3 min.

**2.5 Footnote in place** (EPUB step 7). Do: tap the superscript "1" after "From my grandfather Verus"; Close; tap Back. Expect: an alert titled "Note" with "Annius Verius was his grandfather’s name…"; the same lines stay on screen; Back changes nothing. Record: before and after screenshots. 3 min.

**2.6 Rotation and relaunch** (EPUB step 8). Do: after 2.1, rotate the phone to landscape and back (the highlight may move to another page: turn pages to find it); then force-quit the harness, reopen it, open `se-meditations.epub` again and go to Book I (the harness reopens at the start of the book). Expect: the step-2.1 highlight is on exactly the same words in both orientations and after the relaunch. Record: screenshots in `step-08.md`. 5 min.

**2.7 Reader settings and Literata** (EPUB step 9; human judgement; C3). Do: use A+ and A−, Theme and Face. Expect: A+ and A− change the size in 2 pt steps between 14 and 28 pt; Theme switches Paper and Night with the highlight visible in both; Face cycles Literata, New York, SF Pro, the publisher's font. Literata renders as Literata, with correct italics in the Book I headings: compare with `Docs/Design/images/Main.png` (the design language sheet with the type roles), `Reader.png` and `ReaderDark.png` (Night, with the type settings); there is no separate specimen file. Record: a screenshot of each face; the designer signs off in Session 10. 10 min.

**2.8 The highlight in grayscale** (EPUB step 10; human judgement). Do: Settings > Accessibility > Display & Text Size > Color Filters > Grayscale; look at the highlight in Paper and Night; then turn the filter off. Expect: the underline still sets the highlight apart from plain text. Record: screenshots and the reviewer's name. 5 min.

**2.9 Protected and hostile files** (EPUB step 13).

- Do: from the fixture list open, one after another, `g01-encrypted-chapter.epub`, `g01-lcp-like.epub`, `g01-adept-like.epub`, `g01-traversal.epub`, `g01-bomb.epub` and `g01-truncated.epub`. Expect: each shows a "Cannot open" alert naming the reason (unsupported protection; unsafe archive, with its reason code; truncated); no crash or hang, and the list stays usable.
- Do: open `g01-font-obfuscated.epub`. Expect: it opens and shows two short chapters.
- Do: open `g01-csp-bypass.epub` and page through its three chapters. Expect: each shows its paragraph of text and nothing else happens (no alert, no navigation away).
- Do: open `g01-nesting-bypass.epub` and page to the end. Expect: chapter 2 shows "Before the comment." and "After the comment."; chapter 3 shows "This section cannot be shown: its markup is not XHTML that Sylvia can read safely."; no crash or hang.
- Do: open `g01-form-navigation.epub` (review R2-01), start a screen recording, and in chapter 1 tap with a finger each of the four buttons below the paragraph ("Continue", "Continue to the record", "Continue to the picture", "Continue to the appendix"), waiting a few seconds after each; then page to chapter 2. Expect: after every tap the same chapter 1 page stays on screen, and none of "An archive entry the manifest does not declare.", "A declared XML resource outside the spine.", "A declared XHTML resource outside the spine." or "Markup declared as a PNG image." ever appears; chapter 2 shows "This section cannot be shown: it contains scripts or interactive content." and no buttons.

Record in `step-13.md`: each file's alert or what it showed (screenshots), and the screen recording of the taps (MP4 under 2 MB). The alert wording is harness text, not product wording (plan 4.4.6). 15 min.

**2.10 VoiceOver** (EPUB steps 5 and 6). Do: turn on VoiceOver (Settings > Accessibility > VoiceOver); open `se-meditations.epub`, Book I; with the rotor's Text Selection (or a double-tap-and-hold, then drag) select "From my governor" and open the edit menu; activate Highlight; then tap Saved and swipe through the rows. Expect: VoiceOver can focus and activate Highlight, Note, Look Up and Ask, each announced by its title, and after Highlight announces "Saved" or lets you reach it (step 5); in Saved each row is read as "Quote: <exact text>" followed by "Your note: <note>" when there is one, and swiping moves through every row (step 6). If text selection with VoiceOver is not possible in the reader at all, record failed, with what happened. Record in `step-05.md` and `step-06.md`: the gestures that worked, anything unreachable, and a screen recording with VoiceOver captions or a transcript of what was spoken. Turn VoiceOver off afterwards. 20 min.

**2.11 Open time** (EPUB step 12; Release build from the Home Screen). Do: for each of the five fixtures, five cold opens (force-quit, relaunch, tap), timed from the tap to the first readable page by counting frames of a screen recording at 60 fps. Expect: every run under 2 s (plan 19.2). Record: the table of 25 times. 60 min.

**2.12 The hosted tests on the device** (EPUB step 11; Debug; it replaces the Release app, so after 2.11).

```sh
xcodebuild test -project Spikes/G01-EPUB/G01EPUB.xcodeproj -scheme EPUBHarness -configuration Debug \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G01-EPUB/.build/DerivedData-device-debug \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z \
  -only-testing:EPUBHarnessTests 2>&1 | tee Docs/Stage0/evidence/G01-EPUB/device/device-tests.log
xcrun devicectl device copy from --device "$DEVICE_ID" --domain-type appDataContainer \
  --domain-identifier com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.epub --source Documents/evidence \
  --destination Docs/Stage0/evidence/G01-EPUB/device/
```

Keep the phone idle and on power while it runs, and note its temperature state if it felt warm (the sanitizer timing below). Expect: "Executed 38 tests, with 0 failures" (the filter selected 37 tests when this runbook was written, [`only-testing-ids.log`](evidence/M0-runbook/only-testing-ids.log); the gate's review round 3 added `SanitizerAtCapsTimingTests`, and the simulator runs 38: `evidence/M0-Integration/final-fix/ci-logs/31-g01-epub.log`); in `safety.json`, `"loopback_request_count": 0` for the `sylvia` configuration (the safety test's loopback recorder runs on the phone itself, so that is the device result for publication scripts and remote resources). **Sanitizer time near its caps** (review R3-01): among the 38, `SanitizerAtCapsTimingTests` sanitizes the three slowest documents the adapter still serves three times each and writes `sanitizer-at-caps-device.json` (pulled with the evidence above): every run's seconds, the build configuration, the device and the phone's load average. Expect: every run of every document served and under the 20 s budget (`max_seconds` < 20); the test fails otherwise. This is a Debug build, expected to be slower than Release (not measured); if a run is over 20 s, record **failed** with the times, measure once more and report both, and never raise the budget to make it pass (the caps or the budget are the product owner's: report open question 8). Record: the log and the pulled files; in `step-11.md` the nine times next to the Mac's figures in the report (finding F5). The checklist repeats this step on the oldest supported iPhone, device A, your iPhone 11 Pro, the phone the 20 s budget must hold on (Session 8's repeats; it can wait for M3). 30 min.

**G0.1 PDF.** Install the Debug harness ([checked](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g01-pdf-debug.log); `DEVELOPMENT_TEAM` on the command line signs both targets, which in Xcode means setting the team on PDFSpikeHarness and PDFAnchorKit, as the checklist says). Record all steps in `G01-PDF/device-run.md` and `device-<step>.png`.

```sh
sylvia_generate Spikes/G01-PDF
xcodebuild build -project Spikes/G01-PDF/G01PDF.xcodeproj -scheme PDFSpikeHarness -configuration Debug \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G01-PDF/.build/DerivedData-device \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z
xcrun devicectl device install app --device "$DEVICE_ID" \
  Spikes/G01-PDF/.build/DerivedData-device/Build/Products/Debug-iphoneos/PDFSpikeHarness.app
```

**2.13 Finger selection: lines, columns, rotated pages** (PDF steps 1-3).

- Lines (step 1). Do: open `labels-multipage.pdf` and scroll to page index 3 (printed label 1); long-press a word in the first body line, drag the end handle down to the middle of the third line, then choose **Highlight** in the edit menu. Expect: the status line reads "Saved highlight (n on this file)"; the selection is dismissed and at once (no flash, no visible delay) an ochre fill with a darker ochre underline appears on exactly the selected words on all three lines and nowhere else. Record: `device-1.png`, passed-device or failed, any mismatch, whether the handle landed where your finger aimed (on the simulator, downward drags on the unrotated pages left the selection end 19 to 29 pt, two to three lines at fit zoom, above the touch point, so its test needed corrective drags; simulated touches are not a finger) and whether the highlight appeared at once. Then (G0.1 PDF review rounds 3 and 4, rows 8a and 22a) make two selections that end at the end of a line and highlight each: long-press the last word of the first body line ("water", the end of "The pond in winter ... the water"), and select the whole third body line ("who stops at the shore hears it settle.") by dragging its end handle just past the line's last word. Record whether the handle stayed on that line: on the simulator a drag ending 6 pt past a line's end selected to the line's end, while one ending 40 pt past a left-column line of `two-column.pdf` selected part of the title above instead (row 8a). Before the line-end fix such a selection was saved but could not be restored (report finding 11); 2.14 checks that both come back.
- Columns (step 2). Do: open `two-column.pdf`; start a selection in the last line of the left column ("row like the spines of closed books.") and drag the handle into the first two lines of the right column; Highlight. Expect: the selection follows reading order (the end of the left column, then the top of the right one) without unrelated lines, and the overlay covers the same words. Record: `device-2.png`, and any line the handle could not reach or any extra line selected.
- Rotated pages (step 3). Do: open `rotated.pdf`; on each of page indices 1 (90), 2 (180), 3 (270) and 5 (270 with crop) select two lines with a finger and Highlight. (The simulator made the same selections with simulated touches, report row 2a, and there one drag was enough on each rotated page; this step is a person's finger and eye.) Expect: the handles move along the rotated text, and each overlay sits on the selected words with the underline on the baseline side of the text. Record: `device-3-p<index>.png`; passed-device or failed per page; whether the handle landed where your finger aimed.

Write all three in `device-run.md`. 30 min.

**2.14 Relaunch** (PDF step 4). Do: force-quit the harness, relaunch it and open each file used in 2.13. Expect: the status line reads "Highlights: n stored, n restored exactly" with the same n as saved (on `labels-multipage.pdf` this includes the two selections of 2.13 that end at a line's end), and the overlays reappear in the same places. Record: `device-4.png` and the status text. 5 min.

**2.15 Zoom and landscape** (PDF step 5; human judgement). Do: on `rotated.pdf` page index 1, pinch to about 400 % and pan across a highlight; then rotate the phone to landscape and back. Expect: the overlay stays aligned with the words at every zoom level and in both orientations (tiles redraw sharp after a moment), and no offset grows with zoom. Record: `device-5a.png` (about 100 %), `device-5b.png` (about 400 %) and `device-5c.png` (landscape). 10 min.

**2.16 VoiceOver** (PDF step 6). Do: turn on VoiceOver; on `labels-multipage.pdf` use the rotor or a double-tap-and-hold to select text, then look for the selection actions. Expect: VoiceOver reads the page text; a selection can be made; Highlight is reachable in the edit menu or as the toolbar button announced "Highlight selection", and after it the status line ("Saved highlight ...") is announced or reachable. VoiceOver also reads eight invisible one-point test labels (harness only: link, overlay and selection frames, two probe points, the view frame and, for the bundled fixtures, the values of the text form fields; six when this runbook was written): note them, but they are not a failure of the reader. Record: which method worked (edit menu, toolbar, both, neither), the exact announcements, and a screen recording if practical (`device-6.mov`, under 2 MB). VoiceOver off afterwards. 10 min.

**2.17 Image-only page** (PDF step 7). Do: open `mixed-scanned.pdf`, go to page index 1, try to select text and tap Highlight. Expect: nothing is selectable; the page line shows "No selectable text on this page"; Highlight gives "No selectable text on this page" or "Select text first", never a saved highlight. Record: `device-7.png`. 3 min.

**2.18 Links** (PDF step 8). Do: open `actions.pdf` and tap each of its four links once. Expect: link A and link C (launch) do nothing visible except the "Links:" line showing "blocked"; link B shows "Open link?" with `https://example.invalid/track` (tap Cancel; Safari must not open); link D moves to page index 1. Do: open `actions-nonurl.pdf` and tap links A to I in order, scrolling back to page index 0 after each. Expect: A goes to page index 1 and B to page index 2; C (Find), D (GoToPage) and F (another file) show "blocked" on the "Links:" line and stay on page 0; E (Print), H (JavaScript) and I (submit form) do nothing, and no print sheet or other panel appears; G resets the field below the links to "Default". Do (the gate's review rounds 3 and 4): open `actions-chained.pdf` (every URI and JavaScript action in it aims at the harness's canary address except link D's https link: chained /Next actions, actions in /AA, form field actions; its open action and page 0's open trigger are GoTo actions to page index 2). Expect on opening: the page line reads "Page index 0 of 3" and stays so (the harness scrolls to page 0 itself, so watch for a jump to page index 2 in the first seconds). Tap links A to L in order, scrolling back to page index 0 after each. Expect: A, B, C and G go to page index 1 and F to page index 2, each with nothing added to the "Links:" line; D shows "Open link?" with `https://example.invalid/chained` (tap Cancel); E, K and L each add "blocked" to the "Links:" line and stay on page 0; H, I and J do nothing at all. Then tap each of the three fields below the links, type a few characters and tap the page outside the field: the field shows what you typed (autocorrection may change a word: record what it shows) and nothing is added to the "Links:" line. Then open another file from the Open menu: nothing is added to the "Links:" line. In all three files "URLs opened into the harness: none" throughout, and no other app opens at any point, including when the file is first opened. (The simulator test `LinkTapUITests` makes the same taps, typing a digit into each field; this step repeats them with a finger.) The step's last part, the pointer's hover over links and fields, needs an iPad with a trackpad or mouse: Session 9.7. Record: the "Links:" and "URLs opened" lines, the three fields' text and anything that differs, in `device-run.md`. 20 min.

**2.19 Protected and damaged files** (PDF step 9). Do: open `encrypted-user-password.pdf`, then `truncated.pdf`, from the Open menu. Expect: "This PDF is password-protected. Password-protected PDFs are not supported." and "This PDF is incomplete: the file ends early."; no password prompt; no crash. Record: the two messages in `device-run.md`. 3 min.

**2.20 Open time for a large PDF, in a Release build** (PDF step 10; corrected, C7; [checked](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g01-pdf-release.log)).

```sh
xcodebuild build -project Spikes/G01-PDF/G01PDF.xcodeproj -scheme PDFSpikeHarness -configuration Release \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G01-PDF/.build/DerivedData-device-release \
  -disableAutomaticPackageResolution -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z
xcrun devicectl device install app --device "$DEVICE_ID" \
  Spikes/G01-PDF/.build/DerivedData-device-release/Build/Products/Release-iphoneos/PDFSpikeHarness.app
```

Do: put `~/Library/Caches/SylviaFixtures/generated/g01-pdf/large-1000.pdf` in Files on the phone (AirDrop it and choose Files if asked, or copy it into iCloud Drive's "Sylvia test" folder and Move it to On My iPhone, as in 5.2); launch from the Home Screen; five cold opens through Open > Open from Files…, force-quitting between them; then highlight one word and five more cold opens. Expect: the timing line reads "Open … ms (digest, checks, parse); text of 1000 pages … ms; restore highlights … ms (n restored); first page drawn … ms after display began; total … ms", and "open" plus "restore highlights" plus "first page drawn" is at most 2 s (plan 19.2, p95; the all-pages text extraction is the harness indexing synchronously and is recorded apart). Record: the ten timing lines verbatim, and that the build was Release on phone B, a newer device than the baseline (its result does not stand in for the baseline's). 25 min.

**2.21 Rotated text and stream order** (PDF step 11; report row 10c and finding 12; added on the gate's review branch, here since 19 October 2026). The Release harness of 2.20 serves. Do: open `text-layout.pdf`. On page index 0, select one of the vertical lines ("Upward line one ...") with a finger and Highlight it; do the same for an upside-down line. Then on page index 1 drag from the left column's last line into the right column's first line and Highlight. Force-quit, relaunch and open the file again. Expect: the selections can be made and saved; after the relaunch the status line reads "Highlights: 3 stored, 3 restored exactly". The overlay on the rotated lines is known to be wrong (row 10c, failed on the simulator): the fill covers the line, but the underline is a block at one end of a vertical line and runs along the tops of the glyphs on an upside-down line. On page index 1 the selection follows reading order (the end of the left column, then the top of the right column). Record: `device-11a.png` (page 0 with both highlights) and `device-11b.png` (page 1); whether a finger can select along a vertical line at all, and how the handles behave; the status text after the relaunch. What you see is an input to the approach decision for row 10c (plan 9.2, ADR-0005 open items: a per-line reading direction in the anchor, or accepting the drawing). 15 min.

### Session 3 · Audio on phone B (about 4.25 h of your time, 6.5 h elapsed)

Have the Bluetooth headset (or wired headphones for steps 4 and 7.3) and phone D. Record under `Docs/Stage0/evidence/G04-Audio/device/`: after each step pull the harness log as `step-NN-harness.log` (NN is the checklist step), with `xcrun devicectl device copy from --device "$DEVICE_ID" --domain-type appDataContainer --domain-identifier com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.g04audio --source Documents/g04-harness.log --destination Docs/Stage0/evidence/G04-Audio/device/step-NN-harness.log`; write what you saw and heard, and pass or fail, in `step-NN-notes.txt`; screenshots as PNG. Capture records are JSON files under `Library/Application Support/G04Harness/Captures/captures/` in the same container; pull that folder the same way when a step asks for them.

**The book folder.** The harness plays every audio file in `Documents/G04Book` of its container, offers no way to remove one, and never creates the folder itself (`HarnessModel.bookFromDocuments`); the Player section's title counts the files it found. So check the title after every change below: `G04Book (1 files)` for the M4B alone, `(4 files)` for the four-file book, `(2 files)` in 3.14. If `devicectl` refuses a copy, or the title shows another count (the copy kept an old file or made a subfolder; `devicectl` documents `--remove-existing-content` for folders but not whether a folder is copied into the destination or onto it: [`tool-help.txt`](evidence/M0-runbook/tool-help.txt)), set the folder by hand: Xcode > Window > Devices and Simulators > phone B > G04 Audio > Download Container, put the files in `AppData/Documents/G04Book` inside the downloaded package, then Replace Container; record that you did.

**3.1 Harness and book** (G0.4 step 2; build folder corrected, C32). The Release harness gets a derived-data folder of its own: built in the checklist's `DerivedData-release` after Session 1.4's Release test build, the app it installs still carries the 9.3 GB test bundle ([as written](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g04-harness-release-as-written.log); [corrected](evidence/M0-runbook/device-builds/g04-harness-release.log)).

```sh
xcodebuild build -project Spikes/G04-Audio/G04Audio.xcodeproj -scheme G04AudioHarness -configuration Release \
  -destination "id=$DEVICE_ID" -derivedDataPath Spikes/G04-Audio/.build/DerivedData-release-app \
  -allowProvisioningUpdates -allowProvisioningDeviceRegistration DEVELOPMENT_TEAM=A4KBDX665Z
xcrun devicectl device install app --device "$DEVICE_ID" \
  Spikes/G04-Audio/.build/DerivedData-release-app/Build/Products/Release-iphoneos/G04AudioHarness.app
xcrun devicectl device process launch --device "$DEVICE_ID" com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.g04audio
xcrun devicectl device copy to --device "$DEVICE_ID" --domain-type appDataContainer \
  --domain-identifier com.jevidon.sylvia.spike.g04audio \
  --source ~/Library/Caches/SylviaFixtures/downloads/MeditationsMarcusAurelius_librivox.m4b \
  --destination Documents/G04Book/MeditationsMarcusAurelius_librivox.m4b
```

If `devicectl` reports that `Documents/G04Book` does not exist, copy the M4B as a folder instead, with the 3.14 commands. Force-quit and relaunch the harness. Expect: `Player · G04Book (1 files)`; in the log `output volume=0.01 preview_volume=0.01 reason=default`, a `loaded` line with `duration_ms=17267125`, and `member index=0 duration_ms=17267125 timing=precise`. Tap "Full volume (now quiet, 1 %)". Expect: "Quiet playback (now full volume)" and `output volume=1.0 preview_volume=1.0 reason=toggle`; after any later relaunch the `output` line reads `volume=1.0 preview_volume=1.0 reason=toggle_or_launch_argument` (the setting is kept). Steps 3.2-3.6, 3.13-3.14 and 3.16 need full volume: check the button label before each of them. 15 min.

**3.2 Two hours locked** (G0.4 step 3, T18). Do: play from the start at 1x; note the UTC time just before you lock the phone with the side button; leave it locked, on battery and untouched, for two hours; **listen** at the start, after about an hour and at the end; note the UTC time just before you unlock; unlock, pause, and pull the harness log as `step-03-harness.log`; then run the Python check of G0.4 checklist step 3.4 on it with the two times (for example `2026-11-02T09:00:00Z`): it prints the checkpoint count, the largest gap, the rows written while locked, and the events and checkpoint reasons logged while locked. Expect: continuous audio; checkpoints throughout with the largest gap at most 5.5 s; most rows `protectedDataAvailable=false`; no `checkpoint-failed`; while locked only `checkpoint` events (`reason=periodic`, plus `reason=lifecycle` at the lock, and `reason=itemChange` only for a multi-file book): T18's "no unrelated background work", so any `launch`, `loaded`, `capture`, `export`, `export-resumed` or `session` line, or any other checkpoint reason, while locked fails it (the script prints `events while locked {'checkpoint': N}`); the final `last_ms` within 10 s of about 7,200,000. Record: the script output, first and last checkpoint lines, the Settings > Battery screenshot ("Last 24 Hours", the G04 Audio row: a record, not a pass criterion), battery before and after. 15 min of your time, 2 h 15 min elapsed (Session 10 reading fits here).

**3.3 Headphones removed** (G0.4 step 4, T19; blocks M0: ADR-0006 lists it among its pending device evidence, and its decision 6 is the interruption and route policy that steps 4-6 test; Section 7). Do: at full volume (3.1), connect headphones, play, then unplug them (or switch them off) and **listen** to the speaker; then reconnect them. Expect: playback pauses at once and nothing plays from the speaker; the log has `checkpoint reason=routeChange`; reconnecting does not start playback. Record: the pulled harness log as `step-04-harness.log`, and pass or fail with what you heard in `step-04-notes.txt`. 5 min.

**3.4 Phone call** (G0.4 step 5, T19; phone D; blocks M0, as 3.3). Do: at full volume, play, call phone B from phone D, answer, talk for 20 s and hang up; then the same with playback paused before the call; then once more while playing, declining the call.
